- **acto-fixer**: Applies the MINIMAL fix for exactly ONE pinned divergence found by acto-critic. The failing test pins the divergence; the fix makes that test pass. Never bundles multiple fixes. Dispatch one acto-fixer per blocker issue, serially. Always followed by an acto-critic re-audit. - **adapters**: Framework adapters (`hono`, `edge`, `node`, `astro`, `nuxt` and the rest), the clients (`client`, `client/native`) and the local-first entries (`powersync`, `sqlite`, `tanstack-db`), which are data engines. - **adapters**: An adapter is everything atc knows about one agent CLI. The daemon's session core never sees past the adapter interface, so a new agent CLI is a new adapter, not a change to the core. ALWAYS runs framework-specific adaptive rules, responsive-consistency, and anti-patterns; ALSO runs desktop window-resize (area D1) when the platform includes desktop. Reads the discovery artifact and writes up to four artifacts. Cross findings fold into scored areas by tagging (W*/N*/D1); the anti-pattern step also emits filter verdicts that downgrade/drop weak findings. Adversarial both ways, NO-INVENTED-OBSERVATIONS. - **adaptive-ui-audit-native-analyzer**: Native adaptive/responsive UI analyzer. Runs only for apps with platform=native (React Native / Expo AND Flutter). Reads the discovery artifact and writes four area artifacts with severity + confidence classified findings and file:line evidence. Never applies web/DOM rules. Never invents runtime observations; a visual claim is OBSERVED only if a simulator/emulator render confirms it, else "Static analysis — verify on &lt;device/orientation/font setting&gt;" naming the tool. Never reads source files or writes prose. Stops if no web, native, or desktop UI is detected. Never modifies application code. If no web, native, or desktop UI is found, records PLATFORMS=none so the orchestrator can stop. - **add-orchestrator**: ADD (Assurance-Driven Development) methodology workflow runner. Drives the spec-driven fast path: signed-off spec → bulk-drafted invariants → batched audit → user-triaged findings → approved invariant docs ready for implementation. Dispatches parallel subagents per module. Hands off to byfuglien for verification-chain work and to hellebuyck for ongoing spec governance. Speaks product register, not methodology vocabulary. Triggers: "drive ADD", "ADD fast path", "spec to invariants", "bulk-draft invariants from spec". ([nicholls-inc/crosscheck](https://github.com/nicholls-inc/crosscheck/blob/HEAD/crosscheck/agents/add-orchestrator.md) · ★1)

- **ADR-ANIM-001-part-rig**: All non-AI creature motion is in `core/src/SpriteMotion.cpp` and `core/src/PixelRig.cpp`. Effects are in `core/src/Effects.cpp`. AI key poses are in `core/src/KeyPoses.cpp` and are driven from `app/src/SpriteAnimateDialog.cpp`. ([AceGears/AceStudio](https://github.com/AceGears/AceStudio/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/ADR-ANIM-001-part-rig.md))
- **ADR-ANIM-002-motion-studio**: What can be extended: the part rig is the right base for retargeting. It already keeps bone lengths, layers parts with depth, and renders in a pixel-art-safe way. What is missing: a 3D skeleton type, motion import, a camera model, editable and saved rigs (ADR-ANIM-001 schema 4), keyframed tracks and curves, and a workspace UI. ([AceGears/AceStudio](https://github.com/AceGears/AceStudio/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/ADR-ANIM-002-motion-studio.md))
- **ADR-ANIM-003-motion-pixel-studio**: - **No paid cloud use without the user's approval**, and no public unauthenticated GPU endpoint. Every AI step stays optional, local first, with an explicit cost prompt when it is remote (`docs/agents/CLOUD_GPU_DESIGN.md`). - **Pixel-art safety first.** Nothing may blend colours or add partial alpha to pixel art unless the user asks for it. That is why the in-betweener below is not a neural interp ([AceGears/AceStudio](https://github.com/AceGears/AceStudio/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/ADR-ANIM-003-motion-pixel-studio.md))
- **ADR-ANIM-004-editable-rigs**: The part rig of ADR-ANIM-001 is rebuilt from the sprite every time a clip is made and thrown away afterwards. Users cannot see it, fix a bad cut, move a joint, add a bone for a tail or a held sword, or keep the rig with the project. ADR-ANIM-003 lists saved, editable rigs as the next stage of phase 3. ([AceGears/AceStudio](https://github.com/AceGears/AceStudio/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/ADR-ANIM-004-editable-rigs.md))
- **ADR-ANIM-005-vector-shapes**: The brief asks for vector shapes (a pen with anchors, rectangles, ellipses, auto-close) rasterised pixel-perfectly, dithered gradient fills and stroke styles. AceStudio already has raster Line, Rectangle, Ellipse, Polygon, Curve and Gradient tools (ordered Bayer dithering included), but what they draw cannot be edited afterwards. ([AceGears/AceStudio](https://github.com/AceGears/AceStudio/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/ADR-ANIM-005-vector-shapes.md))
- **ADR-ANIM-006-palette-lock**: Indexed colour mode already limits painting to the palette, but an RGBA project (the default, and what most sprite work uses because of translucent pixels) has nothing that keeps its colours together: smooth gradients, pasted images, generated in-betweens and motion clips, and imported frames all bring new colours, and the palette itself can drift while it is edited. ([AceGears/AceStudio](https://github.com/AceGears/AceStudio/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/ADR-ANIM-006-palette-lock.md))
